### Engine 0.7.0 work package "safety gate, printed words, public files" (coach_answers_round5.md, 2026-10-12)

Layer: `tools/fixture/v07-words.mjs` (applied by both builds after the v0.6 layers). Tests: `tests/v07-words.test.mjs`. Engine 0.7.0.

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| G-270 | **A health-check flag no longer stops the plan** (COACH-R5 R5-GEN-02 "It doesn't stop because we're not taking on any liability, but it should warn them: this is not a plan from a doctor that can be tailored to you by a medical professional. You need to seek a medical professional to make sure that this is okay for you and healthy for you."). `rules.legal.health_flag.on_flag: 'warn'`: a yes on any health question (heart, surgery or fracture in the last 6 months, pregnant or postpartum, an injury in treatment, a doctor's no) builds the plan with status `ok`; `meta.health` carries `warn: true` and what raised it (`reasons`: `refer.reason.health_check` 'a yes on the health check' and / or `refer.reason.severe_pain`; never which health question, G-274); `meta.warnings` carries `warning.health_flag`; the why is `why.health_flag` (R5-GEN-02). The plan prints the warning right under the release line (full and overview views: label + "Why you see this: a yes on the health check" + the warning; block / week / day views: the short line), in English and Spanish, and the "Before you start" stamp reads "you said yes to at least one question" instead of "nothing in it asked for a doctor first". The notice is not printed a second time in the notices list (`render.v7.health_warning_up_front`). A model without `rules.legal.health_flag` keeps the engine 0.6 stop (status `refer`, no weeks), so an older model still behaves as it was built. | `intake.mjs` `safetyScreen(intake, model)`, `healthFlagMode`, `deriveValues`; `generate.mjs` run; `render.mjs` `healthWarning`, `ownRisk`, `ownRiskShort`, the health stamp, `noticesSection` |
| G-271 | **Severe pain: the plan is built with the moderate pain rules and the same warning** (PROPOSED; read from R5-GEN-02 - the coach ruled on the health check, not on severe pain itself). Engine 0.6 always referred on severe pain, even past the coach bypass. Treating it as the strictest pain level the engine plans (moderate: the painful lifts left out, the area's groups held, the pain-free depth cues) keeps one consistent reading of "it doesn't stop, it warns". `rules.legal.health_flag.severe_pain.build_as: 'moderate'`; the derived pain keeps `reported_severity: 'severe'`; `refer.reason.severe_pain` is named in the warning. The intake still accepts `severity: 'severe'`; `rules.volume.pain.refer_if_severity` is unchanged (nothing reaches the planner as severe). Question for the coach: is this right, or should severe pain plan as if that area could not be trained at all? R5-GRD-08 (skip jumps above 6 of 10) belongs to the knee-pain plyo work, not to this layer. | `intake.mjs` `deriveValues` (D7); `validate.mjs` V1 |
| G-272 | **The coach routes** (R3-GEN-01, R2-GEN-03, R5-GEN-02). `health_route: coach_screen`: the client answers the check; a yes builds the plan with the warning, as for a person on their own. `coach_own_sop`: the check is not read (the coach's own procedures), `warning.coach_bypass` prints as before, and a flag on a check that was not read gives no warning; severe pain is still read and gives the warning (`meta.health.bypass` and `warn` both true). `rules.legal.health_flag.coach_routes` records this. The intake fields do not change (`intake/2`). | `intake.mjs` `safetyScreen`; `validate.mjs` V1 |
| G-273 | **The checks** (H20, V1, V21). H20 / V21 (the liability gate) are unchanged and still run first: no accepted release, no plan, whatever the health answers (`generate.mjs` validates the release before the health screen). V1 with `on_flag: 'warn'`: status `refer` is a violation (`refer_retired_health_flag_warns`); an ok plan with reasons or severe pain must carry `meta.health.warn` and `warning.health_flag`; a warn with no reason is a violation; severe pain must reach the plan as `build_as`. V21 adds: a plan with `meta.health.warn` can print every health-warning line and every reason in English and Spanish; V1 also fails a reason that names a health answer (G-274). The registry carries the reading (`validation.checks[V1].v07`, `[V21].v07`). `assertModel` refuses a warn rule whose words are missing or whose release gate is not required. | `validate.mjs` `v1Safety`, `v21Release`; `model.mjs` `assertModel` |
| G-274 | **The plan never says which health question was a yes; the warning wording is legal copy.** The spec keeps health answers out of every plan, link and export (`legal_and_privacy.no_data_kept.health_answers`: 'used on screen for this build only; never in the plan', R3-GEN-01 'I don't want any data kept'). Engine 0.6 never had to: a flag gave no plan. Now that a flagged plan is built, every yes on the health check becomes the one reason `refer.reason.health_check` ('A yes on the health check'); severe pain, a plan input already, is named. V1 fails a plan whose reasons name a health answer (`health_answer_in_plan`). The wording is legal copy (R5-GEN-02 "the warning wording joins the attorney review list"). The four printed lines carry the `[LEGAL REVIEW]` / `[REVISIÓN LEGAL]` marker in the text, like the health-check copy (`health.intro`, `health.cleared`, D-35: legal texts carry their marker until counsel signs off), so a flagged plan shows it until the attorney approves the wording; the attorney review is deferred and does not block. `rules.legal.health_flag.legal_review: true`. The Spanish is machine-written and listed in `es_review.priority_review`. | `tools/fixture/v07-words.mjs` `UI_W7` |
| G-275 | **The core block is printed as optional and adjustable** (COACH-R5 R5-GRD-06 "Make sure you make the core work optional, everything should be adjustable/adaptable"). A block the person switched off prints "(off)" as before, not "optional". The engine still builds the core block first on every training day and never drops it on its own (H05, R4-SES-04). PROPOSED wording. | `render.mjs` `dayCard`, `adjustGuide`; `rules.render.v7.core_optional` |

No comments or threaded-comment authors are stored in the workbooks. | `dumps/`, `analysis/`, `raw/`, `synthesis/02_exercise_catalog_crosswalk.json`, `design/scratch/snap*/`; test `tests/v07-words.test.mjs` |

### Engine 0.7 selection package: templates, selection, athletic progression and WODs (coach answers round 5; 2026-10-13)

Source: `coach_answers_round5.md` (R5-*; newest, with `coach_answers_chat_2026-10-11.md`). Layer `tools/fixture/v07-select.mjs`,
applied after the v0.6 layers by both builds; engine `select.mjs`, `classwork.mjs` (new), `athletic.mjs`, `generate.mjs`, `render.mjs`,
`validate.mjs` (V29 reading). Tests: `tests/v07-select.test.mjs` (named by R5 id). The engine version bump is left to the lead (the
round-5 packages merge into 0.7).

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| G-250 | **2 days a week = two full-body days built from his slots** (COACH-R5 R5-GEN-01 'Two full-body days would be more beneficial'; R4-SES-05 slots; UNI-11 / H06 pairing; CH5-02 slot 5). The 2-day A / B day types of the aesthetics, athletic (in season too), bootcamp-family and no-equipment templates become skeletons `gym_FB1` / `gym_FB2` / `athletic_FB1` / `athletic_FB2`: day 1 keeps the A day's horizontal pull + squat (knee + pull) and takes the B day's vertical / incline push + single-leg hinge (hip + push); day 2 keeps the B day's horizontal push + hinge (hip + push) and takes the A day's vertical pull + lunge (knee + pull). Over the two days both pull and both push vectors, a two-leg and a single-leg knee lift, a two-leg and a single-leg hip lift. Slot 5 is one arm exercise a day (biceps day 1, triceps day 2), slot 6 the leg extension / leg curl; station ids, ranks, pairs and the athletic block are kept. **PROPOSED: which slots go to which day.** The FB skeletons are read like gym_A / gym_B by `selection.skeleton_laterality.lead_row`, V30's partner reading and the benchmark scorer (2.6). Day titles EN + ES. | `amendTemplatesV07`; `rules.selection.v7.two_day_full_body` |
| G-251 | **2 days: full-body day 1's vertical / incline push is the week's 'Chest / Shoulders' slot** (R4-SES-03 'every big group ... about 6-8'; OBSERVED BYOP 'Chest/Shoulders'; G-250 moved the incline slot off the pressing day). With day 1's slot a high-incline press (credited to the shoulders) the chest of sample 08 sat at 5 of 8 ('below 6' line). Day 1's slot now takes a 30-45 degree incline press (`selection.x1.chest_shoulders`; the FB1 skeletons need no lead press on the same day). Sample 08: chest 6 -> 8, shoulders 6 -> 7, every big group inside 6-8, no below-floor line. | `select.chestShouldersSlots`; `rules.selection.v7.two_day_full_body.chest_shoulders_skeletons` |
| G-252 | **Incline first for throwers and volleyball; an overhead press kept for shoulder health** (COACH-R5 R5-ATH-04 'we bias incline pressing for strength, but overhead pressing should be included for health ... to be able to reach up and have that range of motion available'). Baseball, softball, javelin and volleyball profiles (`profile_re`) take the incline-first press like the swimmer (R4-LIB-03, R3-ATH-01). Every such plan, the swimmer's too (`SHOULDER_SWIMMER`), keeps an overhead press: with two or more vertical-push slots a week, the week's last one is an overhead press in every block (`why.overhead_for_health`; the dial treats it as a two-arm press); with ONE such slot a week, the slot keeps its incline press in every block and the week's joint care holds one light overhead press (joint-care code `OVERHEAD_HEALTH`: 2 sets, a day without the vertical-push slot, tall-kneeling / Z / seated machine press first; no muscle-set credit, so no group's sets move; it is dosed as joint care - 2 x 12-15, easy and controlled - and is not a resistance set in the week's totals; the dial and the keeper never read it as a lifting station). Two readings that changed the slot itself between blocks were built and dropped: an overhead press in the last block took the incline press's chest credit away and the weekly chest sets fell (H21, 2- and 3-day thrower plans); an overhead press in the first block put the chest a set and a half above its printed schedule when the incline came back (R4-PRG-01, sample 01). A sport hot spot's avoided presses (the swimmer's standing / seesaw presses) wait for the others. **PROPOSED: one overhead press a week (two slots) or a weekly joint-care press (one slot); the profiles read as throwers and volleyball.** | `rules.selection.v7.incline_overhead` (`keep_overhead`, `one_slot_care`), `rules.prehab.by_injury_code.OVERHEAD_HEALTH`; `select.overheadKeeperSlot`, `pickFor`, `chestShouldersSlots`, `inclineVpushDays`, `decideUpperLaterality`; `week.addPrehab`; `dose.repClassFor`; `volume.isResistance` (a joint-care slot is never a resistance set) |
| G-253 | **Jumps progress two-leg and low -> single-leg and higher** (COACH-R5 R5-GRD-03 'start with jump rope then bilateral high hops, then unilateral easy hops to then hurdle hop overs ... adjust accordingly for athletics'). A lower-body jump record has a level read on its laterality, tags and id: 1 two-leg low (pogo / ankle hops, landings, low box and squat jumps), 2 two-leg higher (hurdle hops, tuck, depth, broad, knee-high boxes), 3 easy single-leg or split-stance, 4 single-leg and higher (single-leg hurdle hops, bounds, skaters). An exercise block may pick up to its number in the plan + 1 (above it: -10 a level); inside that cap a level above the block's own number leans back (-2 a level) so the low jumps lead the first block. **Hard rule: a single-leg hurdle hop is never a pick of the plan's first exercise block** (`jump_stage` filter). Sample 03 block 1: plate hops, ankle hops, two-foot lateral jumps, a squat jump (was single-leg forward hurdle hops from week 1); block 2 (pre-season): bilateral hurdle hops and a single-leg broad jump. **PROPOSED: the four levels, one level a block, the weights.** | `rules.selection.v7.jumps`; `select.jumpLevel`, `hardFilter`, `v7Score` |
| G-254 | **A cutting sport's lateral jump every day, two-foot first** (COACH-R5 R5-ATH-10, confirming G-132). Three two-foot lateral records, regular picks (the answer approves "a two-foot lateral jump"): `two-foot-lateral-jump` (side to side over a line or a low hurdle, ballistic), `two-foot-lateral-jump-onto-box` (sideways onto a low box, ballistic) and `two-foot-lateral-box-jump` (quick lateral step hops over a low step, plyometric - so a lateral station can also hold the week's plyometric share, R2-ATH-01: with only ballistic lateral records the linebacker's week 2 fell to a 0.30 share against 0.365). Each pick is used once a week, so the third training day of a first block has a lateral jump of its own (sample 03 Friday: the lateral box jump, was a squat jump). | `addedExercisesV07` |
| G-255 | **Knee pain: assisted jumps first, and the pain-scale line** (COACH-R5 R5-GRD-08 'don't start with bodyweight plyos, you can start them assisted plyos, also warn against plyos if pain is more than a 6 on a scale 1-10 (10 being you need a hospital)'). Five new records (a squat jump, a two-foot lateral jump and pogo / side-to-side hops holding a rack, rail or counter; a suspension-trainer assisted jump squat; never a band - H01): regular picks for a client with knee pain, nobody else's (hard filter). The first exercise block's jumps are assisted (+30 / -30; one assisted jump a day first - a second one on the same day scores -32, so a day with more jump stations than the library's assisted records takes the lowest two-leg jump for the rest), later blocks take body-weight jumps (an assisted record -30), and the level progression then starts one block later. **Open:** a client whose week holds more jump stations than the four assisted jump-slot / ankle records fills the rest with level-1 two-leg jumps (a low box) - assisted repeats, or fewer stations? A client with pain in an area a landing loads (knee, ankle / foot, hip / groin, low back - PROPOSED list) whose plan holds lower-body jumps reads one line up front (`warning.jump_pain_scale`, EN + ES, the numbers from the rule). The assisted hops (pogo, side to side) are ankle-track records only (`AP_JUMP_LIMITED_KNEE`; as jump-slot picks their plyometric contacts took a ballistic-share plan over its share in the sweep, V27); the jump slot takes the assisted squat jumps. Sample 08: an assisted lateral jump (Monday) and assisted pogo hops (Thursday) weeks 1-4, a two-foot lateral box jump and lateral ankle hops weeks 5-8, a low box jump and zig-zag ankle hops weeks 9-12 (both two-leg and low: with knee pain the level cap is one block behind). | `rules.selection.v7.assisted_jumps`, `jump_pain_warning`; `select.hardFilter`, `v7Score`; `generate.buildMeta` |
| G-256 | **Athletic doses hold at most 2 weeks and reach the block's final number** (COACH-R5 R5-ATH-02, approving the proposal). A family total of the stairway (jumps, throws, cuts, sprint metres) holds at most 2 weeks (a run of 3 equal weeks); a longer run is split from its end (its last week takes one dose step, never above the week after it, its own target or the share band), else from its start (one step back, never below the week before); the block's last week is never lowered. The todo test R02-cod-and-throws-climb is converted (below). | `rules.athletic.v7.hold`; `athletic.planAthletic` |
| G-257 | **Named benchmark workouts in the class workout** (COACH-R5 R5-GRD-04 'use the WODs that are popular in the crossfit community, girl wods once a week and hero wods only 1 a block, popular benchmark wods every 2-4 weeks, keep it varied'). A library of 20 named workouts (13 "Girls", 4 "Heroes", 3 other benchmarks) with the community's standard prescriptions and, per level (L1 / L2 / L3), the reps, rounds, time cap, movement options and loads (never by sex - H13). Each week one WOD day becomes a Girl workout (the day rotates); a Hero comes once a training block (three quarters in, on the week's longest WOD); a benchmark every 3 weeks, moved to the next week when that week holds the Hero (so the gap stays inside 2-4 weeks); the other WODs stay the engine's own. A workout is offered only when every movement resolves to an exercise the kit, the age rules, the pain and the switches allow (scaled options first for the levels that take them), never a counted heavy squat or hinge (H07), no full Olympic lift below L3, wall balls at most 20 lb (H11) and, for a youth or a 60+ client, the ball's top less 2 lb; the age and injury rules are read exactly as V4 / V5 / H12 read them (60+: never a snatch or depth work, older-ok or opt-in-ok movements only; an injured / high-volume client never the never-tags; a youth stays on the youth ladder - the first sweep found a 66-year-old's Isabel scaled to a DB snatch and a 15-year-old's Diane with pike push-ups). HYROX is not a class workout. The plan prints the name, the standard and the scaled line. A workout none of whose movements repeats an exercise (or a near-duplicate) already on its day is taken (the core block's sit-ups and Annie; a jumping pull-up and Murph's pull-ups). Sample 04 (8 weeks): a Girl every week (Diane, Elizabeth, Isabel, Karen, Nancy, Kelly, Chelsea, Fran), Murph in week 6, The Chief in week 3 and Death by Burpees in week 7 (week 6 holds the Hero), all at the client's level (L2, scaled options where the library writes them). **PROPOSED: the library, the scaling and loads by level, the Hero's week, every 3 weeks.** | `rules.conditioning.v7.named_wods`; `classwork.applyNamedWods` |
| G-258 | **The WOD run station** (R5-GRD-04): `run-wod-station` (reference-only, picked by no slot) is the run of Helen, Nancy, Kelly, Murph and Michael; with no running space the WOD takes a calorie machine instead. | `addedExercisesV07` |
| G-259 | **The bootcamp's two formats** (COACH-R5 R5-GRD-05 'vary bootcamp into circuit training not just horizontal set by set per muscle but sometimes ... in rounds'). The week's circuit days alternate "in rounds" (through all stations: his vertical training) and "station by station" (all sets of a station, then the next: his horizontal training), and each exercise block flips them, so every week holds both (a week with one circuit day takes the formats by block). The circuit line, the station note and the dose ('4 sets x 35 s' / '4 rounds x 35 s') say which; one line up front (`warning.bootcamp_format`). No muscle set moves (a station keeps its rounds as sets). **PROPOSED: the split.** | `rules.supersets.v7.bootcamp_format`; `classwork.applyBootcampFormat`; `render.formatReps`, `dayCard` |

### Engine 0.7.0 sweep fix round: the five random intakes failing an error check (2026-10-13)

Source: `node tools/sweep.mjs 1500 --render` after the 0.7.0 merge (G-300): V27 on random-451, random-878 and random-940 (since the 0.7
selection package, the R5-ATH-02 hold pass), H21 + H22 on random-1332 (since the 0.7 volume package, the G-219 bonus-week raise), on both
models. Root causes in the engine; no check changed, no rules key added. Engine: `volume.mjs`, `athletic.mjs`. Tests: `tests/v07-sweep.test.mjs`
(one describe per rule id, both models). Engine version stays 0.7.0.

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| G-301 | **A bonus week is never raised above what a later week can hold** (H21 'weekly sets never decrease'; R4-PRG-01; G-219). The bonus-opening search records a week's minimum (`weekMin`: the template's lifts at their tops plus the bonus's fewest sets) while the block's final sets still allow it; a later round of the same search may lower the later weeks' caps (`gCap`: what the lifts hold without a bonus that never opens), and `fitM` then raised that one week above every later week (random-1332, chest 11 11 11 12 12 12 12 11 11 11 11 11). The raise is now capped by the lowest cap of every later week, so the schedule stays non-decreasing by construction. random-1332: chest 11 every week (capped at 11 of 13, said in the plan as before). | `volume.mjs` `fitM` |
| G-302 | **The weeks' targets are the schedule the block keeps** (H22 'every week follows the planned schedule'). The end-of-block reconciliation levels a schedule so that no week sits above the week after it; that levelling did not mark the schedule as changed, so the weeks kept the targets they were recorded with. A step the levelling takes back now writes the weeks' targets again, as a lowered week always did. A safety net behind G-301. | `volume.mjs` (block reconciliation) |
| G-303 | **The hold rule never raises plyometric work in a week over its plyo share** (R2-ATH-01 share; R5-ATH-02 'may hold for up to 2 weeks'; V27 `share_over`). The 0.7 hold pass reverted a step only when the week was inside its share band before it, so a week already over its share (random-878 RC2 weeks; random-940's stage-3 weeks, all repeated-contact drills by design, share 100 %) took a reactive or ankle step up - the plyometric work then sat above its minimum dose while the share was over. A hold step now never takes a week further outside its band, and a week over its share takes no reactive / ankle step up at all (a ballistic step that lowers the share is still allowed). | `athletic.mjs` hold pass (`bandOff`, `overTop`) |
| G-304 | **The hold rule splits a plateau without lengthening the next one** (R5-ATH-02; R3-VOL-04 'natural growth throughout'; V27 `early_peak`, E-166). The pass stepped the last week of an over-long run up to the week after it, and repeated, so with one dose step left (a single landing station at 3 x 7 -> 3 x 8) a 6 + 6 split became 3 + 9 and the block's peak held 9 of 12 weeks (random-451, a 61-year-old's year plan). A step that meets the week after it is now taken only when the run it joins stays inside the hold (`max_hold_weeks` + 1); else the run's first week gives a step back as before. random-451 block 1: 18, 21 x 5, 24 x 6. | `athletic.mjs` hold pass (`joined`) |

Checks after the round: tests 1,684 (1,679 pass, 0 fail, 3 to-do, 2 skipped; 16 new); sweep 1,578 x 2 with `--render`: 0 failing error
checks, 0 throws, 0 render problems on both models; samples: only 08 changes (its athletic doses, `samples/STATUS_v0.5.md`); benchmark
`--label v07` against `results_v06.json`: mean 98.04, min 89.35, hard violations 2 in 1 plan, 60 plans pass every acceptance row (all as
G-300); against the previous `results_v07.json` 15 plans change, 13 at the same score, bench-21 91.61 -> 91.55 (share_off one more week)
and bench-63 95.10 -> 94.94 (athletic_attainment one more miss) - the price of a hold pass that no longer overdoses the share or lengthens
the peak.

The model label moves to 1.4.0: model 1.3.0 plus the coach's round-5 answers (`coach_answers_round5.md`) and his chat answers CH5
(`coach_answers_chat_2026-10-11.md`) and CH6 (`coach_answers_chat_2026-10-13.md`), all of them already applied by the v0.6 and v0.7
layers (G-100 ... G-304; `coverage_matrix_round5.md`). The engine stays 0.7.0. Test: `tests/v07-release.test.mjs`.

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| G-305 | **A release label does not change a plan** (INFERRED; the coach graded the sample plans, R5-GRD-01 ... 08). The seed of every tie-break hashes the intake with the model version (CONTRACTS §1.3). Bumping the label to 1.4.0 with nothing else reshuffled the picks of all 8 samples (a different seal row, lunge, curl, triceps lift, ankle hop, medball throws ...), with no rule behind the change. `rules.determinism.seed_version_map` maps 1.4.0 to 1.3.0 (and the fixture's 1.4.0-fixture to 1.3.0-fixture); `generate.mjs` seeds from the mapped version. The 8 samples are byte-identical except `model_version` and the printed 'Model 1.4.0' line. A later model version that should reseed simply leaves the map out. | `rules.determinism` (`tools/fixture/v07-words.mjs`); `generate.mjs` run; `tests/helpers/stub-derived.mjs` stubSeed |

Tests converted: `compile.test.mjs` 'loadModel works on the compiled model' and `review-r3.test.mjs` 'R3-engine-version' read model 1.4.0 (were 1.3.0).


### Performance pass: faster set placement, identical plans (2026-10-13)

The public web tool calls `generate()` once per plan in the browser, where samples 06 and 07 took 12-17 s. A pure refactor: every
plan, and its rendered text in English and Spanish, is byte-identical to engine 0.7.0 before the pass. Checked with
`tools/perf/plan-hashes.mjs` (SHA-256 of the plan JSON and of the full EN / ES render, every sample and benchmark intake, generated
in EN and in ES, on the compiled model and the reference fixture: 408 of 408 identical) and with a same-process A/B against the
old engine on 756 random sweep intakes (300 random + 52 sports + 26 activities, on both models: 756 identical). Same-process times,
old -> new (busy 4-core machine): sample 06 12.9 s -> 1.6 s, 07 16.0 s -> 1.4 s, bench 09 25.6 s -> 1.5 s, bench 06 23.6 s -> 1.7 s, the
year plan (bench 13) 17.2 s -> 2.7 s; the 408 hash runs 1,204 s -> 298 s in all. What is left on the slowest plans (year plans, fat-loss
year plans) is selection (`pickFor` / `hardFilter` / `scoreOf`) and the checks, not the set placement. `ENGINE_VERSION` stays '0.7.0': the plan JSON carries
`engine_version`, so a bump would change every plan's bytes for no change of behaviour.

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| G-311 | **The set placement keeps what it has already worked out** (performance only; no rule changes). (1) The credits of an exercise in a bucket and their group / line sums are memoised per model; a slot's credits, credit class and lead lift are kept with the pick they were read for. (2) Every write to the block's sets and picks goes through one writer that moves a state version; the credit tallies, the press / pull totals (kept as exact running sums - every credit is 1, 0.5 or 0.25 and every set a whole number), the group slot lists and the day lists are read again only after a write. (3) A scratch run (`reachable`) is keyed by everything the allocation reads - the sets, last days, owners, picks, day lists, the bound week's three readings, every switch and table - and answered from the memo when the same question comes again, with the targets, back-room check and balance flag the run would leave behind. (4) Inside one `allocate`, a run that starts from the same state with the same targets replays the previous run's `addSet` answers while its calls match one for one. (5) The candidate order reads each sort key once per candidate (same comparator, same order); a list of one is not sorted. (6) Selection compiles each rule pattern once; tie-break hashes and the forbidden-key / third-party-link walks of the checks avoid repeated work. (7) Browser support: the engine uses nothing newer than Safari 15.0 - the `(?<!leg )press` lookbehind in `render.mjs` (which made the module fail to parse on Safari before 16.4) is an explicit loop with the same matches, and `Array.prototype.at`, `Object.hasOwn` and `structuredClone` in `engine/progression/` are written out; `tests/v07-browser.test.mjs` scans for them. | `volume.mjs` (runBlock: `write`, `live*`, `reachMemo`, `replay`, `tallyOf`), `select.mjs` (`reOf`), `util.mjs` (`tieBreak`, `forbiddenKeys`), `validate.mjs`, `render.mjs`, `progression/*.mjs`; `tools/perf/plan-hashes.mjs` |
| G-312 | **A swap is its own edit** (`engine/edit.mjs` op `swap_exercise {weekday, slot_id, exercise_id, weeks}`; COACH-CX CX-02 / CX-04 "it should be all editable as they need to", COACH-R4 R4-LIB-04). The web tool's *Swap* button needs a swap that keeps the line where it is; `remove_exercise` + `add_exercise` would move it to the end of a block, give it a new slot id and borrow another line's dose. So the line keeps its slot id, block, position, sets, reps, effort, rest, progression rule, bucket and superset; the exercise's own fields are the new exercise's: name, cue, video (the YouTube search, R4-LIB-05), laterality, group and credits (`model.credits(exercise, the line's bucket)`, H08; a line that counted no sets - athletic work, H09 - still counts none; a carry or a never-credit exercise counts none and a carry is dosed by trips, R4-LIB-02). A note that named the old pick as a stand-in (`note.instead_of`, `note.pain_instead_of`) is dropped; the other notes stay. The swap list becomes the line's first exercise, then the one just replaced, then the rest (never the new one), with `swapNotes` against the new pick, and the item carries `swapped_from` (the line's first exercise; removed when it is swapped back). Totals and the summary are recomputed and the plan is marked edited as for every edit. The web tool swaps in every week that holds the same exercise in that line (`app/edit-ui.js > swapEdits`). Generated plans are unchanged: no engine version bump. | `edit.mjs`; `tests/edit-swap.test.mjs`; `app/edit-ui.js` |
